## 3.1 Procedure

Th<sub>e par</sub>ti<sub>c</sub>i<sub>pan</sub>t<sub>s were</sub> fi<sub>rs</sub>t <sub>presen</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>an</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orme</sub>d <sub>consen</sub>t f<sub>orm w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> th<sub>ey were</sub> f<sub>am</sub>ili<sub>ar</sub>i<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>purpose o</sub>f this research<sub>, p</sub>rocedure<sub>, p</sub>otential risks and ri<sub>g</sub>hts associated with their data. For eac<sup>h</sup> image, t<sup>h</sup>ey <sup>h</sup>a<sup>d</sup> to express t<sup>h</sup>eir ju<sup>d</sup>gment o<sup>f</sup> t<sup>h</sup>e <sup>f</sup>o<sup>ll</sup>owing statement "T<sup>h</sup>is image was create<sup>d</sup> wit<sup>h</sup> t<sup>h</sup>e <sup>h</sup>e<sup>l</sup>p o<sup>f</sup> generative AI". ## 3.2 Stimuli Generation

We chose the initial pool of 320 facial images from the standardized Stable Difusion Face Dataset <sup>1</sup>, generated via Stable Difusion 1.5, 2.1, and SDXL 1.0. The images were generated using simple prompts such as "photo of a woman" and <sub>un</sub>d<sub>erwen</sub>t <sub>a</sub> <sub>qua</sub>lit<sub>y</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>l <sub>c</sub>h<sub>ec</sub>k <sub>as</sub> <sub>we</sub>ll <sub>as</sub> <sub>a</sub>dditi<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>e</sub>diti<sub>ng</sub> t<sub>o</sub> fi<sub>x</sub> <sub>any</sub> <sub>s</sub>k<sub>ewe</sub>d f<sub>ac</sub>i<sub>a</sub>l f<sub>ea</sub>t<sub>ures</sub> b<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>au</sub>th<sub>or</sub> <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> database. All ima<sub>g</sub>es have a resolution of 512 × 512 <sub>p</sub>ixels and are subse<sub>q</sub>uentl<sub>y</sub> ins<sub>p</sub>ected for artifacts (e.<sub>g</sub>., facial as<sub>y</sub>mmetries, anatomical distortions). This curated ima<sub>g</sub>e set served as the in<sub>p</sub>ut for our validation task in Stud<sub>y</sub> 1. An exam<sub>p</sub>le subset is de<sub>p</sub>icted in Fi<sub>g</sub>ure 2.

## 3.3 Sample Size Determination

Sam<sub>p</sub>le size <sub>p</sub>lannin<sub>g</sub> was <sub>g</sub>uided b<sub>y</sub> the <sub>g</sub>oal of achievin<sub>g</sub> ade<sub>q</sub>uate <sub>p</sub>recision for continuous 0–100 AI-likeness ratin<sub>g</sub>s [18, 32]. Each ima<sub>g</sub>e was to be classified as “reliabl<sub>y</sub> AI-lookin<sub>g</sub>” when the lower bound of the 95% confidence interval around its mean ratin<sub>g</sub> exceeded 60 <sub>p</sub>oints [76]. To ensure that confidence intervals were suficientl<sub>y</sub> narrow not to cross this threshold, we tar<sub>g</sub>eted an avera<sub>g</sub>e mar<sub>g</sub>in of error of a<sub>pp</sub>roximatel<sub>y</sub> � = 6 <sub>p</sub>oints on the ratin<sub>g</sub> scale, consistent with <sub>p</sub>ractical constraints on <sub>p</sub>artici<sub>p</sub>ant load.

Assumin<sub>g</sub> a standard deviation of � = 20 <sub>p</sub>oints, consistent with <sub>p</sub>rior <sub>p</sub>erce<sub>p</sub>tual ratin<sub>g</sub> studies [18], the re<sub>q</sub>uired <sub>num</sub>b<sub>er o</sub>f <sub>ra</sub>ti<sub>ngs per</sub> i<sub>mage was ca</sub>l<sub>cu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>as:</sub>

$$
n = \left( \frac { t _ { \alpha / 2 } \times \sigma } { E } \right) ^ { 2 } = \left( \frac { 1 . 9 6 \times 2 0 } { 6 } \right) ^ { 2 } \approx 4 0 .\tag{1}
$$

Thus<sub>,</sub> a<sub>pp</sub>roximatel<sub>y</sub> 40 ratin<sub>g</sub>s <sub>p</sub>er ima<sub>g</sub>e were re<sub>q</sub>uired to achieve the desired level of <sub>p</sub>recision. Given 180 total ima<sub>g</sub>es and 40 ratin<sub>g</sub>s <sub>p</sub>er <sub>p</sub>artici<sub>p</sub>ant<sub>,</sub> we estimated that recruitin<sub>g</sub> around 175 <sub>p</sub>artici<sub>p</sub>ants would <sub>p</sub>rovide com<sub>p</sub>lete stimulus covera<sub>g</sub>e while maintainin<sub>g</sub> inde<sub>p</sub>endent observations across raters. The final dataset included 169 <sub>p</sub>artici<sub>p</sub>ants <sub>a</sub>ft<sub>er</sub> <sub>qua</sub>lit<sub>y</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>l<sub>,</sub> <sub>y</sub>i<sub>e</sub>ldi<sub>ng</sub> 6<sub>,</sub>760 <sub>va</sub>lid t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>a</sub>l<sub>s</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>mee</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>p</sub>l<sub>anne</sub>d l<sub>eve</sub>l <sub>o</sub>f <sub>prec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on</sub> f<sub>or</sub> <sub>re</sub>li<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> i<sub>mage</sub> <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ass</sub>ifi<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> E<sub>mp</sub>i<sub>r</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y,</sub> th<sub>e average</sub> h<sub>a</sub>lf<sub>-w</sub>idth <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> 95% <sub>con</sub>fid<sub>ence</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erva</sub>l <sub>across</sub> i<sub>mages was</sub> 4<sub>.</sub>8 <sub>ra</sub>ti<sub>ng po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>s, con</sub>fi<sub>rm</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t the achieved <sub>p</sub>recision was within the tar<sub>g</sub>eted mar<sub>g</sub>in of error.

## 3.4 Participants

We recruited 178 <sub>p</sub>artici<sub>p</sub>ants (� = 178) throu<sub>g</sub>h Prolific Academic. The sam<sub>p</sub>le included 97 men (54.5%), 77 women (43.3%), 3 non-binar<sub>y p</sub>artici<sub>p</sub>ants (1.7%), and 1 <sub>p</sub>artici<sub>p</sub>ant who self-described their <sub>g</sub>ender. Partici<sub>p</sub>ants’ a<sub>g</sub>es ran<sub>g</sub>ed from 20 to 78 <sub>y</sub>ears (� = 37.49, �� = 12.45). The a<sub>g</sub>e distribution was broadl<sub>y</sub> re<sub>p</sub>resentative of an adult <sub>p</sub>o<sub>p</sub>ulation, with 18–24 <sub>y</sub>ears (14.7%), 25–34 <sub>y</sub>ears (40.1%), 35–44 <sub>y</sub>ears (18.6%), 45–54 <sub>y</sub>ears (16.5%), 55–64 <sub>y</sub>ears (7.1%), and 65+ <sub>y</sub>ears (2.9%). Most <sub>p</sub>artici<sub>p</sub>ants re<sub>p</sub>orted En<sub>g</sub>lish (EN) as their surve<sub>y</sub> lan<sub>g</sub>ua<sub>g</sub>e (99.7%), and the most re<sub>p</sub>resented countries were Australia (10.9%), Canada (8.2%), the United States (7.6%), South Africa (5.3%), and the United Kin<sub>g</sub>dom (5.0%).

After a<sub>pp</sub>l<sub>y</sub>in<sub>g</sub> <sub>q</sub>ualit<sub>y</sub>-control criteria (see Section 3.5), where we we excluded nine s<sub>p</sub>eeders (median <sub>p</sub>er-trial time < 4.45 s)—169 <sub>p</sub>artici<sub>p</sub>ants (� = 169) and 6,760 valid trials were retained for anal<sub>y</sub>sis.

## 3.5 Data Processing

All <sub>p</sub>re<sub>p</sub>rocessin<sub>g</sub> and anal<sub>y</sub>ses were conducted in P<sub>y</sub>thon. Trials with missin<sub>g</sub> or non-numeric values in Rating or Time were discarded. To ensure data <sub>q</sub>ualit<sub>y</sub>, we im<sub>p</sub>lemented a multi-ste<sub>p</sub> filterin<sub>g</sub> <sub>p</sub>rocedure combinin<sub>g</sub> trial-level and <sub>p</sub>artici<sub>p</sub>ant-level checks. At the trial level, univariate outliers were identified and removed for Time, Confidence, and Rating usin<sub>g</sub> an inter<sub>q</sub>uartile-ran<sub>g</sub>e criterion (1.5 × ��� be<sub>y</sub>ond the 25th or 75th <sub>p</sub>ercentile). At the <sub>p</sub>artici<sub>p</sub>ant level, we excluded individuals who showed at<sub>yp</sub>icall<sub>y</sub> short com<sub>p</sub>letion times (“s<sub>p</sub>eeders,” defined as a median <sub>p</sub>er-trial time below the 5th <sub>p</sub>ercentile of the <sub>g</sub>rou<sub>p</sub> distribution, corres<sub>p</sub>ondin<sub>g</sub> to < 4.45 s), <sub>p</sub>artici<sub>p</sub>ants with ver<sub>y</sub> low variabilit<sub>y</sub> in res<sub>p</sub>onses (“flatliners,” defined as havin<sub>g</sub> both ratin<sub>g</sub> SD and IQR below 5), and <sub>p</sub>artici<sub>p</sub>ants contributin<sub>g</sub> fewer than 20 valid trials. This filterin<sub>g</sub> <sub>p</sub>rocedure excluded nine <sub>p</sub>artici<sub>p</sub>ants (all classified as s<sub>p</sub>eeders), resultin<sub>g</sub> in 169 retained <sub>p</sub>artici<sub>p</sub>ants out of 178 total (95%) and 6,760 valid trials out of 7,120 total (95%).

Ima<sub>g</sub>es below th<sub>e</sub> 60<sub>-po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t th<sub>res</sub>h<sub>o</sub>ld h<sub>a</sub>d l<sub>ower</sub> <sub>mean</sub> <sub>ra</sub>ti<sub>ngs</sub> $( M = 4 8 . 1 , S D = 1 0 . 3 )$ and wider confidence intervals (mean width = 14.5 <sub>p</sub>oints).

RQ1: Does the actual source of a face (AI vs. Human) modulate its electrophysiological processing across early attentional (N2), evaluative (P3), and sustained afective (LPP) sta<sub>g</sub>es?

RQ2: Does the congruence between a face’s source and its label—rather than the source itself—drive ERP responses, such that label–face conflict is reflected in com<sub>p</sub>onents indexin<sub>g</sub> conflict detection (N2) and stimulus re-evaluation (P3)?

RQ3: Do disclosure labels alter how a face is visually explored (the number and duration of fixations) and does this d<sub>epen</sub>d <sub>on</sub> th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>ace</sub>’<sub>s</sub> <sub>ac</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>source</sub>?

RQ4: Does source or labeling afect the earliest allocation of attention, as indexed by the latency of the first fixation?

## 4.1 Study Design

We em<sub>p</sub>lo<sub>y</sub>ed a 2 × 3 within-<sub>p</sub>artici<sub>p</sub>ant ex<sub>p</sub>erimental desi<sub>g</sub>n, with the inde<sub>p</sub>endent variables Source (two levels: AI-generated, Human) and Label (three levels: No Label, Made with AI, Made by a human). Source referred to the actual ori<sub>g</sub>in of the facial ima<sub>g</sub>e, whereas Label indicated the <sub>p</sub>resented information label (or absence thereof), delivered usin<sub>g</sub> minimalistic white rectan<sub>g</sub>le holdin<sub>g</sub> the res<sub>p</sub>ective label.

To control for <sub>p</sub>otential order and carr<sub>y</sub>over efects<sub>,</sub> we im<sub>p</sub>lemented a balanced Latin s<sub>q</sub>uare desi<sub>g</sub>n across <sub>p</sub>artici<sub>p</sub>ants [74]. This <sub>p</sub>rocedure ensured that each of the six ex<sub>p</sub>erimental conditions a<sub>pp</sub>eared e<sub>q</sub>uall<sub>y</sub> often in each serial <sub>p</sub>osition <sub>an</sub>d <sub>prece</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d <sub>an</sub>d f<sub>o</sub>ll<sub>owe</sub>d <sub>every o</sub>th<sub>er con</sub>diti<sub>on an equa</sub>l <sub>num</sub>b<sub>er o</sub>f ti<sub>mes across</sub> th<sub>e samp</sub>l<sub>e.</sub>

and ensure consistent viewin<sub>g</sub> distance. An E<sub>y</sub>eLink 1000 Plus e<sub>y</sub>e tracker was calibrated usin<sub>g</sub> a standard 9-<sub>p</sub>oint <sub>ca</sub>lib<sub>ra</sub>ti<sub>on proce</sub>d<sub>ure.</sub>

The main task consisted of 240 trials, or<sub>g</sub>anized in a 2 × 3 within-subjects desi<sub>g</sub>n mani<sub>p</sub>ulatin<sub>g</sub> the actual source of each face (AI-<sub>g</sub>enerated vs. Human) and the associated label (No Label, Made with AI, Made b<sub>y</sub> a human)

## 4.7 ERP Preprocessing & Analysis

EEG <sub>p</sub>re<sub>p</sub>rocessin<sub>g</sub> was <sub>p</sub>erformed usin<sub>g</sub> MNE-P<sub>y</sub>thon and followed established <sub>p</sub>i<sub>p</sub>elines for ERP research. First<sub>,</sub> we automaticall<sub>y</sub> identified and inter<sub>p</sub>olated bad or outlier channels usin<sub>g</sub> the Random Sam<sub>p</sub>le Consensus (RANSAC) method [4], em<sub>p</sub>lo<sub>y</sub>in<sub>g</sub> s<sub>p</sub>herical s<sub>p</sub>line inter<sub>p</sub>olation based on Perrin’s al<sub>g</sub>orithm [56]. A notch filter at 50 Hz was <sub>app</sub>li<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> <sub>remove</sub> li<sub>ne</sub> <sub>no</sub>i<sub>se,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> <sub>s</sub>i<sub>gna</sub>l <sub>was</sub> b<sub>an</sub>d<sub>-pass</sub> filt<sub>ere</sub>d b<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ween</sub> 1<sub>–</sub>15 H<sub>z</sub> t<sub>o</sub> i<sub>so</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>requency</sub> <sub>range</sub> relevant for co<sub>g</sub>nitive ERP com<sub>p</sub>onents. Data were then re-referenced to the common avera<sub>g</sub>e reference (CAR).

Artifact correction was <sub>p</sub>erformed usin<sub>g</sub> Inde<sub>p</sub>endent Com<sub>p</sub>onent Anal<sub>y</sub>sis (ICA) with the extended Infomax al<sub>g</sub>orithm [43]. Com<sub>p</sub>onents corres<sub>p</sub>ondin<sub>g</sub> to e<sub>y</sub>e blinks, e<sub>y</sub>e movements, and muscle noise were automaticall<sub>y</sub> identified and removed usin<sub>g</sub> the “ICLabel” <sub>p</sub>lu<sub>g</sub>in inte<sub>g</sub>rated with MNE [44].

The continuous EEG si<sub>g</sub>nal was se<sub>g</sub>mented into e<sub>p</sub>ochs ran<sub>g</sub>in<sub>g</sub> from 200 ms before to 1000 ms after stimulus onset. A 200 ms <sub>p</sub>re-stim<sub>u</sub>l<sub>u</sub>s baseline <sub>w</sub>as a<sub>pp</sub>lied for baseline correction. We foc<sub>u</sub>sed on three ERP com<sub>p</sub>onents of interest: N2<sub>,</sub> P3 <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> LPP<sub>.</sub>

For the N2<sub>,</sub> we <sub>q</sub>uantified mean am<sub>p</sub>litude in the 200–350ms time window<sub>,</sub> com<sub>p</sub>risin<sub>g</sub> the electrodes Fz<sub>,</sub> Cz<sub>,</sub> FC1<sub>,</sub> FC2 [12, 31, 53]. For P3, mean am<sub>p</sub>litude was <sub>q</sub>uantified in the 300–500 ms window for the P3 and the 400–800 ms window for the LPP [14, 58], time-locked to ima<sub>g</sub>e onset. Based on <sub>p</sub>rior literature [29, 67], we selected a centro-<sub>p</sub>arietal re<sub>g</sub>ion of interest (ROI) com<sub>p</sub>risin<sub>g</sub> electrodes $\mathrm { P z } , \mathrm { C P z } , \mathrm { C z } , \mathrm { C P 1 } , \mathrm { C P 2 } .$ <sub>,</sub> P3 <sub>an</sub>d P4 f<sub>or</sub> P3 <sub>an</sub>d $\mathrm { P z } , \mathrm { C P z } , \mathrm { C z } , \mathrm { C P 1 } , \mathrm { C P 2 } , \mathrm { P 1 }$ <sub>an</sub>d P2 f<sub>or</sub> LPP <sub>e</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>are</sub> t<sub>yp</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> <sub>o</sub>b<sub>serve</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> <sub>response</sub> t<sub>o</sub> <sub>emo</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>an</sub>d <sub>source-re</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>ac</sub>i<sub>a</sub>l i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orma</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> Th<sub>ese</sub> com<sub>p</sub>onents were extracted for statistical com<sub>p</sub>arison across ex<sub>p</sub>erimental conditions (Source × Label) in our statistical modelin<sub>g,</sub> as <sub>p</sub>resented in Section 5.

All <sub>ana</sub>l<sub>yses were per</sub>f<sub>orme</sub>d <sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>a</sub>l l<sub>eve</sub>l<sub>, so</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>w</sub>ithi<sub>n-par</sub>ti<sub>c</sub>i<sub>pan</sub>t <sub>var</sub>i<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y was mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d <sub>ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an average</sub>d awa<sub>y</sub>. Electro<sub>p</sub>h<sub>y</sub>siolo<sub>g</sub>ical and e<sub>y</sub>e-trackin<sub>g</sub> data were anal<sub>y</sub>zed within a sin<sub>g</sub>le Ba<sub>y</sub>esian hierarchical framework im<sub>p</sub>lemented in brms [7], which estimates <sub>g</sub>rou<sub>p</sub>- and individual-level <sub>p</sub>arameters jointl<sub>y</sub> and returns a full <sub>p</sub>osterio M<sub>a</sub>n<sub>usc</sub>ri<sub>p</sub>t <sub>su</sub>bmitt<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> ACM

for ever<sub>y</sub> efect rather than a decision a<sub>g</sub>ainst a threshold [36, 63, 73]. This suits the <sub>p</sub>resent desi<sub>g</sub>n for two reasons. Our <sub>cen</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>l <sub>c</sub>l<sub>a</sub>i<sub>m concerns an a</sub>b<sub>sence o</sub>f dif<sub>erence</sub> b<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ween</sub> AI<sub>- an</sub>d h<sub>uman-source</sub>d f<sub>aces, an</sub>d <sub>a pos</sub>t<sub>er</sub>i<sub>or concen</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>near</sub> <sub>zero</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t di<sub>rec</sub>tl<sub>y,</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>ereas</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>non-s</sub>i<sub>gn</sub>ifi<sub>can</sub>t t<sub>es</sub>t d<sub>oes</sub> <sub>no</sub>t di<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>ngu</sub>i<sub>s</sub>h <sub>a</sub>b<sub>sence</sub> <sub>o</sub>f <sub>an</sub> <sub>e</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>t f<sub>rom</sub> <sub>a</sub>b<sub>sence</sub> <sub>o</sub>f evidence [16]. The N2 thus a<sub>pp</sub>ears sensitive to the <sub>p</sub>resence of a <sub>source</sub> l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l <sub>ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> t<sub>o</sub> l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>–</sub>f<sub>ace</sub> <sub>con</sub>fli<sub>c</sub>t <sub>per</sub> <sub>se.</sub>

## 7.2 Electrophysiological Signatures of Source and Label

Actual source left no credible ERP proxy. In RQ1 we asked whether a face’s actual source, AI-generated or human, modulates its electro<sub>p</sub>h<sub>y</sub>siolo<sub>g</sub>ical <sub>p</sub>rocessin<sub>g</sub> at the earl<sub>y</sub> attentional (N2), evaluative (P3), and sustained afective (LPP) sta<sub>g</sub>es. Actual source left no measurable trace on an<sub>y</sub> of the three com<sub>p</sub>onents. AI- and human-sourced faces <sub>p</sub>roduced e<sub>q</sub>uivalent am<sub>p</sub>litudes<sub>,</sub> with each <sub>p</sub>osterior centered on zero. ## 7.4 ERPs and Gaze Agree on When Disclosure Takes Efect
